Active refinement details:
```
def _active_gradients(arr, indices):
    n=arr.size; grad=np.zeros(n)
    for j in indices:
       i_min=max(0,j-n+1); i_max=min(n-1,j)
       if i_max<i_min: continue
       i_arr=np.arange(i_min,i_max+1)
       p=j-i_arr
       # p within 0..n-1 by boundaries
       seg=arr[i_arr]
       val=arr[p]
       grad[i_arr]+=val
       grad[p]+=seg
    return grad
```
This uses fancy indexing many times. K up to 12, n up to 4096, 48k indices, okay. Could optimize using convolution but fine.

`_actual_score(arr)`:
```
def _actual_score(arr):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01: return float('inf')
    a = a / s
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')
```

```
def _set_family_cfg(n, M):
   theta = ...
```

`_family_shape_from_params(x)`:
```
def _family_shape_from_params(x):
    M = len(x)-1
    base_param = x[0]
    base = 0.05 + 1.9 / (1.0 + math.exp(-np.clip(base_param, -50, 50)))
    sines = _CFG['sines']
    a = base + sines @ x[1:1+M]
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    total = a.sum()
    if not finite or total <=1e-12: return None
    return a/total
```
Use np.exp for scalar. X maybe numpy array. Good.

```
def _family_objective(x):
    a = _family_shape_from_params(x)
    if a is None: return 1e100
    return _actual_score(a)
```

Active refinement function:
```
def _refine_active(a, budget_s, A_est=2.0, K=10):
    a = _normalize(a)
    best=a; best_score=_actual_score(a)
    start=time.monotonic()
    n=a.size
    # set of index transformations? 
    improvements=0
    while time.monotonic()-start < budget_s:
        # compute conv
        c = _fft_convolve(a)
        c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
        cmax = float(c.max())
        score_all_pred = A_est * n * cmax  # because sum 1
        m_est = best_score/(A_est*n) if best_score finite and A_est>0 else cmax
        if not finite m_est or m_est <=0: m_est=cmax
        # decide upper
        if score_all_pred > best_score * 1.15:
            upper = max(m_est * 1.35, cmax*1e-6)
            lower = m_est * 0.2
            # candidate under upper
            cand = np.where(c <= upper + 1e-15)[0]
            if cand.size == 0:
                cand = np.argsort(c)[::-1][:K]
            else:
                # top K under upper
                # sort candidates by c descending
                cvals = c[cand]
                if cand.size <= K: act=np.array(cand)
                else:
                    top = np.argpartition(cvals, -K)[-K:]
                    act = cand[top]
        else:
            act = np.argsort(c)[::-1][:K]
        # ensure act not empty; include mirrors
        act = _include_mirrors(act, n)
        # maybe limit K*2
        # compute gradient
        grad = _active_grad(a, act)
        g = grad - np.mean(grad)
        gmax = np.max(np.abs(g))
        if gmax <= 1e-15: break
        improved=False
        # candidate step scales: exponent target maybe [0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0]
        for expo in (0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
            if time.monotonic()-start > budget_s: break
            step = expo * (g / gmax)
            # To avoid huge negative exponent, clip
            step = np.clip(step, -2.5, 2.5)
            newa = a * np.exp(-step)
            newa = np.where(np.isfinite(newa), newa, 0.0)
            newa = np.clip(newa, 0.0, 1000.0)
            s = newa.sum()
            if s <= 1e-12: continue
            newa = newa / s
            sc = _actual_score(newa)
            if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                best = newa.copy(); best_score = sc; a=newa; improved=True; improvements+=1; break
            # also try reverse sign? Sometimes subgradient ascent needed? To reduce max, decrease high grad; if not improve, increasing high grad won't reduce but might balance due normalization? The mean-adjusted with positive step always decreases high. Could try smaller expo only.
        if not improved:
            # try a random perturbation step with same active? Or random small
            # maybe if no improve for several, break or reduce K
            # Try one random active coordinate scaling?
            if time...:
               random factors for active? 
            # break if too many no improve
            no_improve +=1
            if no_improve > 20: break
    return best,best_score
```
No lambda. Need no variable closures. OK.

`_include_mirrors`:
```
def _include_mirrors(indices, n):
    if n<=1: return indices
    size=2*n-1
    out=set(indices.tolist())
    for j in out? can't modify while. Use list.
    for j in list(out):
       out.add(size-1-j)
    return np.array(list(out), dtype=int)
```

`_active_grad` as above. Note if active includes both mirror, gradient contributions duplicate mirror lags; if objective full max includes both, average same. If objective one side, could over-include; okay.

Step direction: We multiply by exp(-step) where step=(expo/gmax)*g. Coordinates with high gradient decreased. But if active gradient high at coordinates that are already small? gradient product a_neighbor*a_i maybe. Good.

Line search: We try exponents increasing; if small step not improve but larger does? Non-smooth; we break on first improve. Could be better to try all in order from small to large and keep best candidate even if not immediate improve? We can evaluate all and choose if any <best. But more evals. We'll do for expo in list, update local best if <best_score, but continue? If find improve, accept best so far and break to save. Fine.

If no improvement, maybe try `step = -g`? That would increase high-gradient coordinates, likely increasing active c. But due mean, maybe move mass from low to high? unlikely. Could help if active gradient selection wrong. We can occasionally random shuffle.

Add random local search after active if time. Function `_random_refine(a, budget_s)`:
```
def _random_refine(a, budget_s, seed):
    a=normalize; best=a; best_score=actual
    rng=default_rng(seed)
    start=time.monotonic()
    temp=0.3
    while time...:
       n=a.size
       # choose block size maybe max(1,n//50)
       m=int(rng.integers(5, min(n,200)))
       idx=rng.choice(n,m,replace=False)
       factors=np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.4,size=m))
       newa=a.copy(); newa[idx]*=factors; newa=normalize
       sc=actual(newa)
       if sc<best_score: accept; temp=max(0.05,temp*0.99)
       else: maybe reject; temp decay; if no improve 100 break? 
    return best,best_score
```
`rng.choice` for each iteration can be slow; use `rng.integers(0,n,size=m)` and unique? For small. Or `idx = rng.permutation(n)[:m]`. Permutation n=4096 each iter maybe overhead but okay for 1000. Use `idx = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)`. 1000*200 random okay.

Let's draft code in analysis.

```
"""
Black-box search for low-peak step/sequence.
Uses actual evaluate_sequence as oracle. Starts with anti-palindromic Fourier family, optimizes low-dimensional coefficients via Powell, then active-set gradient refinement inferred from score, and random multiplicative local search. Returns best valid nonnegative sequence found within budget.
"""
import numpy as np
import time
import math
try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize as _scipy_minimize
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False

_CFG = {'n': 2048, 'M': 4, 'sines': None}
_TIME_GUARD = {'hard': 0.0}
```

Need top-level `_actual_score`. Inside, if `evaluate_sequence` global? Use:
```
def _actual_score(arr):
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0: return inf
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a,0,1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01: return inf
    a = a/s
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence(a.tolist()))
    except NameError:
        return _internal_score_all(a)
    except Exception:
        return inf
```
But if NameError at compile? `evaluate_sequence` is a global name not defined at definition, but only at call. Fine.

`_internal_score_all(a)`: use FFT conv factor2. Need `_fft_convolve`.

```
def _next_pow2(m): ...
def _fft_convolve(a): ...
def _internal_score_all(a):
   a=np.asarray... normalize? assume; if s<0.01 inf; c=_fft_convolve(a); return 2*a.size*max(c)/(s*s)
```

Family functions:
```
def _configure_family(n, M):
   n=int(n); M=int(M)
   theta = 2*np.pi*(np.arange(n)+0.5)/float(n)
   sines = np.empty((M,n), dtype=np.float64)
   for m in range(1,M+1): sines[m-1]=np.sin(float(m)*theta)
   _CFG['n']=n; _CFG['M']=M; _CFG['sines']=sines
```
Could precompute for same n? M fixed 4. Use M=4. `D=5` including base. Should M=5? D=6. More dims slower. Previous one mode; higher modes 2,3,4 could help. Use M=4.

`_family_shape_from_params(x)`:
```
def _family_shape_from_params(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if x.size < _CFG['M']+1: x = np.pad...
    x = x[:_CFG['M']+1]
    x = np.clip(x, -4.0, 4.0)
    base = 0.05 + 1.9 / (1.0 + math.exp(-x[0]))
    M = _CFG['M']
    a = base.copy()
    # use linalg: a = base + _CFG['sines'].dot(x[1:1+M])
    a = base + _CFG['sines'] @ x[1:1+M]
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-9:
       return None
    return a/s
```
If x[0] large negative, base 0.05, coefficients could negative >, clip. Good.

```
def _family_objective(x):
    a = _family_shape_from_params(x)
    if a is None: return 1e100
    return _actual_score(a)
```

`_make_family_starts(D, seed)`:
```
def _make_family_starts(D, seed=12345):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    starts=[]
    start1=np.zeros(D); start1[1]=0.7; starts.append(start1)
    start2=np.zeros(D); start2[1]=-0.6; starts.append(start2)
    for i in range(3):
       x = rng.uniform(-0.6,0.6,size=D)
       # bias first mode positive
       x[1] = abs(rng.normal(0.0,0.45)) + 0.15
       starts.append(x)
    return starts
```
If objective better with higher modes, random.

`_constant_sequence(n)`: ones/n.

`_active_grad(arr, indices)`:
As above. Need indices as array. Ensure unique.
```
def _active_grad(arr, indices):
   n=arr.size; grad=np.zeros(n,dtype=np.float64)
   for jj in indices:
      j=int(jj)
      i_min = 0 if j < n-1? Actually max(0, j - (n-1))
      i_max = n-1 if j >= n-1 else j
      if i_max < i_min: continue
      i_arr = np.arange(i_min, i_max+1, dtype=np.int64)
      p = j - i_arr
      # p can be negative? with i_min ensures p <=n-1? Let's verify: j - i_max <= n-1 if i_max >=j-(n-1); p>=0 if i_min>=j-n+1; okay.
      vi = arr[i_arr]
      vp = arr[p]
      grad[i_arr] += vp
      grad[p] += vi
   return grad
```
This loops over each i and adds both. If active includes j and mirror, gradient double for same physical lag? For full max objective, c_j and c_{2n-2-j} equal; average includes both, gradient twice identical. That's fine (just scaled active count). If active has K high but mirrors, okay.

`_include_mirrors(indices,n)`:
```
def _include_mirrors(indices, n):
   if n <=1 or indices.size==0: return indices
   size=2*n-1
   arr=np.unique(indices)
   mirrors=size-1-arr
   return np.unique(np.concatenate([arr, mirrors]))
```

`_select_active(c, best_score, n, K, restricted)`:
```
def _select_active(c, best_score, n, K, restricted):
    cmax = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    factor=2.0
    m_est = best_score/(factor*n) if np.isfinite(best_score) and n>0 else cmax
    if not finite or m_est <= 1e-300: m_est = cmax
    if restricted:
       score_all = factor*n*cmax
       if score_all > best_score*1.15:
           upper = m_est*1.45
           lower = m_est*0.05
           eps = 1e-15
           cand = np.where((c <= upper + eps) & (c >= lower - eps))[0]
           if cand.size == 0:
              cand = np.where(c <= upper*2 + eps)[0]
           if cand.size == 0:
              cand = np.arange(c.size)
           # choose top K in cand by c
           if cand.size <= K: return cand.copy()
           vals=c[cand]
           top=np.argpartition(vals, -K)[-K:]
           return cand[top]
    # fallback top K overall
    if c.size <= K: return np.arange(c.size)
    top=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    return top
```
`argpartition` with K maybe > size; handle.
If restricted but actual score lower due factor 2, good. If best_score inf? not call.

`_refine_active(a, budget_s, restricted, seed)`:
```
def _refine_active(a, budget_s, restricted=True, K_auto=True):
   a = _normalize(a)
   if a is None: return a, inf
   best=a.copy(); best_score=_actual_score(a)
   if not finite or budget_s <=0: return best,best_score
   start=time.monotonic(); n=a.size
   K = 10 if n <=2048 else 16
   no_improve=0
   expo_list=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8,1.5)
   while time.monotonic()-start < budget_s:
      c = _fft_convolve(a)
      c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
      if c.size==0: break
      act = _select_active(c, best_score, n, K, restricted)
      act = _include_mirrors(act,n)
      if act.size==0: break
      # limit act to min(max(2*K, ?), n? ) maybe if mirrors expand.
      if act.size > 2*K:
          # keep top by c
          vals=c[act]
          keep=np.argpartition(vals, -2*K)[-2*K:]
          act=act[keep]
      grad = _active_grad(a, act)
      g = grad - np.mean(grad)
      gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
      if gmax <= 1e-18: break
      improved=False
      for expo in expo_list:
          if time.monotonic()-start >= budget_s: break
          step = expo * (g / gmax)
          step = np.clip(step, -2.0, 2.0)
          newa = a * np.exp(-step)
          newa = np.where(np.isfinite(newa), newa, 0.0)
          s = float(newa.sum())
          if not finite or s <=1e-12: continue
          newa = newa/s
          sc = _actual_score(newa)
          if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
             best = newa.copy(); best_score=sc; a=newa; no_improve=0; improved=True
             break
      if not improved:
          no_improve +=1
          # Try a smaller random multiplicative perturbation in active? Maybe to escape.
          if no_improve > 25:
              break
   return best,best_score
```
Potential issue: If restricted active set excludes actual max due factor error, gradient may reduce wrong lags and improve? If not, no improve and break after 25. Then run top-all variant.

```
   n=a.size; no_improve=0; temperature=0.35

```

```
idx = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)

sc = _actual_score(newa)
if sc < best_score - 1e-12: accept, no_improve=0, temperature=max(0.05, temperature*0.99)
```

```
   arr_norm = _normalize(arr); if None continue
   sc=_actual_score(arr_norm)
   if sc<best_final_score: out=arr_norm
return out.tolist()
```

`_fft_convolve`: For n=1, need=1, N=1; rfft length1; okay.
```
def _fft_convolve(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   n=a.size
   if n==0: return np.array([])
   need=2*n-1
   N=1
   while N<need: N <<=1
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)
   return c[:need]
```

```
# ensure symmetric? c symmetric, w symmetric up to rounding; symmetrize: w = 0.5*(w + w[::-1])? Since c symmetric, but due FFT rounding. We can symmetrize.

L=c.size
y = w[::-1].copy()
N = next_pow2(n + L -1)
fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); fy=np.fft.rfft(y,N); h=np.fft.irfft(fa*fy,N)
# h length N, need indices
mid = L-1
Tp = h[mid - np.arange(n)]
Tm = h[mid + np.arange(n)]
grad = Tp + Tm
return grad
```

Mirror update using LSE gradient:
```
c = fft_convolve(a); score=2n*max(c)

g = grad - mean(grad)
gmax=max(abs(g)); if gmax=0 break
for target in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8,1.5]:
    newa = a*exp(-clip(target*g/gmax, -2,2))
    normalize
    sc=internal_score(newa)
    if sc<best: accept; break
```

This LSE gradient is a strong optimizer. Let's implement top-level `_lse_gradient(a, beta)`:
```
def _lse_gradient(a, beta):
    n=a.size
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if not finite: return zeros
    x = beta * (c - cmax)
    x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(x)
    wsum = float(w.sum())
    if not finite or wsum <=0: w = np.ones(len(c), dtype)/len(c)
    else: w /= wsum
    # ensure nonnegative
    # symmetrize? c should be symmetric but numerical; sym to maintain real derivative for self conv.
    w = 0.5*(w + w[::-1])
    wsum = w.sum(); if >0 w/=wsum
    L = w.size
    y = w[::-1]
    need = n + L - 1
    N = _next_pow2(need)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fy = np.fft.rfft(y, N)
    h = np.fft.irfft(fa * fy, N)
    idx = (L - 1) - np.arange(n)
    T = h[idx]
    grad = 2.0 * T
    return grad
```
Need check h index: `h` length N, convolution of a length n and y length L. The valid h indices 0..n+L-2. `mid=L-1=2n-2`, idx from 2n-2 down to n-1. Max 2n-2 < need (3n-2). Good. For i=n-1, idx=n-1. Good.

Potential sign: We want minimize max c. grad positive proportional to neighbor. Try a *= exp(-step*grad) => decreases coordinates whose neighbor weighted high. Good.

Let's test mentally on anti-sine near central: weights peak around max lags (center and quarter). Gradient at positions whose opposite neighbors are high; update reduces products. Good.

Could the LSE gradient cause all mass to concentrate in zeros (spikes) increasing some c? Line search by max score prevents; may find local minima. Starting smooth. Add random restart.

Now low-dimensional Fourier family too but internal objective fast. We can optimize using scipy with internal objective faster. Also can directly run LSE gradient from one-mode initial for many n. The LSE gradient is high-dimensional and may escape one-mode.

Maybe a more systematic approach:
1. For each n, initialize anti-sine with amplitude near optimum (0.707). Run LSE mirror descent for a time budget, tracking best internal score.
2. Also optimize Fourier family using internal objective (fast) to get good start; then LSE refine.
3. Choose best n.
4. Final validate with actual evaluate_sequence.

```
def _random_mult(a, seed, sigma=0.3):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n=a.size
    m = int(max(10, n//20))
    idx = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False) unless n small
    factors = np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,size=m))
    newa=a.copy(); newa[idx]*=factors; return _normalize(newa)
```

Let's implement LSE optimizer carefully:
```
def _lse_optimize(a0, budget_s, seed=0, verbose=False):
    start = time.monotonic()
    a = _normalize(a0)
    if a is None: return None, inf
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = _internal_score(best)
    n = a.size
    no_improve = 0
    it = 0
    target_list = (0.02, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.35, 0.6, 1.0, 1.6)
    # To avoid too many candidate scores, maybe first try medium, then if fail smaller/larger
    while time.monotonic() - start < budget_s:
        c = _fft_convolve(a)
        c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
        cmax = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        if not finite cmax: break
        score = 2.0 * n * cmax  # sum1
        if score < best_score: best=... (should not)
        beta = 12.0 / cmax if cmax > 0 else 1.0
        beta = min(beta, 30.0 * float(n))  # cap? 12/cmax ~ if cmax very small, cap.
        w = _softmax_weights(c, beta)
        grad = _weighted_gradient(a, w)
        # Maybe combine with top active gradient? Not needed.
        g = grad - np.mean(grad)
        gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
        if gmax <= 1e-18: break
        cand_best = None; cand_score = best_score
        # Try a few target exponents. To reduce cost, order by likely: 0.2,0.1,0.5,0.05,1.0,0.4
        for target in (0.2,0.1,0.5,0.05,1.0,0.4,0.8,1.6):
            if time.monotonic() - start >= budget_s: break
            step = target * (g / gmax)
            step = np.clip(step, -3.0, 3.0)
            newa = a * np.exp(-step)
            newa = np.where(np.isfinite(newa), newa, 0.0)
            ss = float(newa.sum())
            if not finite or ss <=1e-12: continue
            newa = newa / ss
            sc = _internal_score(newa)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cand_score:
                cand_score = sc; cand_best = newa
        if cand_best is not None and cand_score < best_score - 1e-13:
            a = cand_best; best = a.copy(); best_score = cand_score; no_improve=0; it +=1
            # optionally print every 100
        else:
            no_improve += 1; it +=1
            # Try random perturbation every 5 no improves or if no_improve > 20
            if no_improve >= 5:
                pert_a = _random_perturb(a, seed + it, 0.35)
                pert_score = _internal_score(pert_a)
                if pert_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                    a=pert_a; best=a.copy(); best_score=pert_score; no_improve=0
                elif no_improve > 80:
                    break
    return best, best_score
```
But `it` increments after no improve; seed uses. Random perturb uses new numpy RNG each call; overhead small.

Let's think about lower bound and possible optimum. The convolution sum identity: ∑_{j=0}^{2n-2} c_j =1. There are 2n-1 lags, average 1/(2n-1).
```
def _internal_score(a):
    arr=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    if arr.size==0: return inf
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr,0)
    arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
    s=float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<0.01: return inf
    arr=arr/s
    c=_fft_convolve(arr)
    if c.size==0: return inf
    mx=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx): return inf
    return float(2.0*arr.size*mx/(s*s))? Wait after arr normalized, s was original. If we use arr after normalization but s original, score formula 2n*max(c_norm)/(s_norm^2). If we normalize arr sum=1, s_norm=1. If using original s, c after normalization uses arr/s, max_c = c_original/s^2, score=2n*c_original/s^2 =2n*max(c_norm). So return 2n*max_c_norm. Simpler: after normalize, return 2n*max(c).
```
In `_internal_score`, after `arr=arr/s`, compute c and return 2*n*max(c). Good. If original sum not1, correct.

`_normalize`:
```
def _normalize(a):
    arr=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    if arr.size==0: return None
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr),arr,0.0)
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<1e-12: return None
    arr=arr/s
    return arr
```

```
def _make_family_starts(D, seed=12345):
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   starts=[]
   x=np.zeros(D); x[1]=0.70710678; starts.append(x)
   x=np.zeros(D); x[1]=0.55; starts.append(x)
   x=np.zeros(D); x[1]=0.85; starts.append(x)
   for _ in range(4):
      x = rng.uniform(-0.7,0.7,size=D)
      x[1] = abs(rng.normal(0.0,0.4)) + 0.35
      # maybe ensure base x0 near 0
      x[0] = rng.normal(0,0.3)
      starts.append(x)
   return starts
```
D=6; first index base.

`_optimize_family_internal`:
```
def _optimize_family_internal(n, M, starts, maxfev):
   _configure_family(n,M)
   D=M+1
   best_a=None; best_score=inf
   for idx,start in enumerate(starts):
       if not HAVE_SCIPY:
          x=start
       else:
          try:
             res=_scipy_minimize(_family_objective_internal, start, method='Powell', options={'maxiter': 200, 'maxfev': maxfev, 'disp': False})
             x=res.x
          except Exception:
             x=start
       a=_family_shape_from_params(x)
       if a is None: continue
       sc=_internal_score(a)
       if sc<best_score: best_score=sc; best_a=a.copy()
   if best_a is None: best_a=_anti_sine(n,0.7)
   return best_a,best_score
```
`_family_objective_internal` returns `_internal_score(a)`. It normalizes, maybe double normalize. Fine.

Phase A maxfev: `maxfev = 400 if n<=4096 else 250 if n<=8192 else 200`.

`_softmax_weights(c,beta)`:
```
def _softmax_weights(c,beta):
   cmax = float(np.max(c))
   x = beta*(c-cmax)
   x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 0.0)
   w = np.exp(x)
   s = float(w.sum())
   if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       w = np.ones(c.size, dtype=float)/c.size
   else:
       w /= s
   w = 0.5*(w + w[::-1])
   s = float(w.sum())
   if s>0: w/=s
   return w
```
If cmax=0 (all zero), beta? handle in optimizer.

`_weighted_gradient(a,w)`:
```
def _weighted_gradient(a,w):
    n=a.size; L=w.size
    need = n + L - 1
    N=_next_pow2(need)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    fy=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1],N)
    h=np.fft.irfft(fa*fy,N)
    idx = (L-1) - np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    T = h[idx]
    grad = 2.0*T
    return grad
```
Need `w` real. Good.

Check gradient sign by testing constant: w peaked central. For constant a_i=1/n. T_i = ∑_{p=0}^{n-1} 1/n w_{i+p}. Since w central at L-1, T_i max in middle. gradient high middle. Update decreases middle, increases edges. Good.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    total=max(120.0, float(budget_s))
    hard=t0+total-30.0
    candidate_scores=[]
    seeds = int(seed)
    M=5
    D=M+1
    lengths=[2048,4096,8192]
    if total > 1800: lengths.append(16384)
    # If budget very small, only 2048
    if total < 600: lengths=[2048]
    # Phase A family
    family_shape_by_n={}
    for n in lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > hard - 600: break
        maxfev = 500 if n<=4096 else 300 if n<=8192 else 200
        starts = _make_family_starts(D, seeds + n)
        a, sc = _optimize_family_internal(n, M, starts, maxfev)
        if a is not None:
            family_shape_by_n[n]=a
            _add_candidate(candidate_scores, a, sc, f"family{n}")
    # If no family, defaults
    for n in lengths:
       if n not in family_shape_by_n:
          a=_anti_sine(n,0.707); sc=_internal_score(a); family_shape_by_n[n]=a; _add_candidate(...)
    # Choose chosen lengths
    largest=lengths[-1]
    # sort lengths by family score
    def? no lambda. Use list of tuples and sort.
    scored = [(family_score_by_n.get(n, internal), n) for n in lengths] but need scores stored. We can store family_score_by_n dict.
```

```
def _score_normalized(a):
   n=a.size
   s=float(a.sum())
   if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=1e-12: return inf
   if s != 1.0: a = a/s
   c = _fft_convolve(a)
   if c.size==0: return inf
   mx=float(np.max(c))
   if not finite: return inf
   if mx < 0: mx=0.0
   return 2.0*float(n)*mx
```

```
def _lse_optimize(a0, budget_s, seed=0, label="lse"):
    a = _normalize(a0)
    if a is None or budget_s <= 0: return a0, _internal_score(a0) if a0 is not None else np.inf
    best = a.copy(); best_score = _score_normalized(a)
```

Iteration:
```
while time.monotonic()-start < budget_s:
    c = _fft_convolve(a)
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    if c.size == 0: break
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if not finite or cmax <= 0: break
    # beta factor
    if no_improve < 8: bf=10.0
    elif no_improve < 20: bf=20.0
    else: bf=40.0
    beta = min(bf / cmax, 10.0 * bf * float(n))
    if beta <= 0: beta = 1.0 * n
    w = _softmax_weights(c, beta)
    grad = _weighted_gradient(a, w)
    g = grad - np.mean(grad)
    gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
    if not finite or gmax <= 1e-18: break
    best_cand=None; best_cand_score=best_score
    for target in (0.15, 0.4, 0.05, 1.0, 0.8, 2.0, 0.02):
       if time.monotonic()-start >= budget_s: break
       step = target * (g/gmax)
       step = np.clip(step, -3.0, 3.0)
       newa = a * np.exp(-step)
       newa = np.where(np.isfinite(newa), newa, 0.0)
       newa = newa / newa.sum() if sum>0 else continue
       sc = _score_normalized(newa)
       if finite and sc < best_cand_score:
          best_cand_score=sc; best_cand=newa.copy()
    if best_cand is not None and best_cand_score < best_score - 1e-13:
       a=best_cand; best=a.copy(); best_score=best_cand_score; no_improve=0; iters+=1
       if iters%100==0: print(...)
    else:
       iters +=1; no_improve+=1
       if no_improve % 10 == 0:
          pert = _random_perturb(a, int(seed + iters), 0.25)
          psc = _score_normalized(pert) if pert is not None else inf
          if finite and psc < best_score -1e-12:
             a=pert; best=a.copy(); best_score=psc; no_improve=0
       if no_improve > 100:
          break
```

```
def _add_candidate(cands, a, score, name):
    if a is None: return
    arr = _normalize(a)
    if arr is None: return
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        score = _internal_score(arr)
    if not np.isfinite(score): return
    cands.append((float(score), len(cands), str(name), arr))
```

```
    elif n >= 8192:
       if middle_count >=2: continue
       middle_count +=1
    real = _actual_score(arr)
    if finite and real < best_real:
       best_real=real; best_out=arr; print
       if best_real < 1.45: break
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step sequences with small normalized self-convolution
peak:
    score(a) = 2*n*max_j(a*a)_j / (sum(a)^2)
under the convention that a is normalized to sum 1.

The search uses a fast FFT surrogate that matches evaluate_sequence for valid
non-negative inputs.  Starts are taken from anti-palindromic Fourier families,
then refined by multiplicative mirror descent on a smoothed max objective
(log-sum-exp over convolution coefficients).  Lengths 2048-16384 are explored.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize as _scipy_minimize
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False

_CFG = {'n': 2048, 'M': 5, 'sines': None}


def _next_pow2(m):
    n = 1
    while n < m:
        n <<= 1
    return n


def _fft_convolve(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    need = 2 * n - 1
    N = _next_pow2(need)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    return c[:need]


def _internal_score(a):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return float('inf')
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
        return float('inf')
    arr = arr / s
    c = _fft_convolve(arr)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float('inf')
    mx = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return float('inf')
    if mx < 0.0:
        mx = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * float(arr.size) * mx)


def _score_normalized(a):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return float('inf')
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return float('inf')
    if abs(s - 1.0) > 1e-8:
        arr = arr / s
    c = _fft_convolve(arr)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float('inf')
    mx = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return float('inf')
    if mx < 0.0:
        mx = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * float(arr.size) * mx)


def _actual_score(a):
    arr = _normalize(a)
    if arr is None:
        return float('inf')
    try:
        val = float(evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        return _internal_score(arr)
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return float('inf')
    return val


def _normalize(a):
    if a is None:
        return None
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    arr = arr / s
    return arr


def _constant_sequence(n):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)


def _anti_sine(n, amp):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    theta = 2.0 * np.pi * (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    a = 1.0 + float(amp) * np.sin(theta)
    return _normalize(a)


def _configure_family(n, M):
    n = int(n)
    M = int(M)
    theta = 2.0 * np.pi * (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    sines = np.empty((M, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for m in range(1, M + 1):
        sines[m - 1] = np.sin(float(m) * theta)
    _CFG['n'] = n
    _CFG['M'] = M
    _CFG['sines'] = sines


def _family_shape_from_params(x):
    sines = _CFG['sines']
    M = int(_CFG['M'])
    if sines is None:
        return None
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if x.size < M + 1:
        x = np.concatenate([x, np.zeros(M + 1 - x.size, dtype=np.float64)])
    x = x[:M + 1]
    x = np.clip(x, -4.0, 4.0)

    base = 0.05 + 1.9 / (1.0 + float(np.exp(-x[0])))
    a = base + sines.dot(x[1:1 + M])
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return _normalize(a)


def _family_objective_internal(x):
    a = _family_shape_from_params(x)
    if a is None:
        return 1e100
    return _internal_score(a)


def _make_family_starts(D, seed=12345):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    starts = []

    x = np.zeros(D, dtype=np.float64)
    x[1] = 0.7071067811865475
    starts.append(x)

    x = np.zeros(D, dtype=np.float64)
    x[1] = 0.55
    starts.append(x)

    x = np.zeros(D, dtype=np.float64)
    x[1] = 0.85
    starts.append(x)

    for _ in range(4):
        x = rng.uniform(-0.7, 0.7, size=D).astype(np.float64)
        x[0] = float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.3))
        x[1] = abs(float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.4))) + 0.35
        starts.append(x)

    return starts


def _optimize_family_internal(n, M, starts, maxfev):
    _configure_family(n, M)
    D = M + 1
    best_a = None
    best_score = float('inf')

    for idx, start in enumerate(starts):
        x = np.asarray(start, dtype=np.float64).copy()

        if HAVE_SCIPY:
            try:
                res = _scipy_minimize(
                    _family_objective_internal,
                    x,
                    method='Powell',
                    options={
                        'maxiter': 250,
                        'maxfev': int(maxfev),
                        'disp': False,
                    }
                )
                x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            except Exception:
                x = np.asarray(start, dtype=np.float64)

        a = _family_shape_from_params(x)
        if a is None:
            continue
        sc = _internal_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_a = a.copy()

    if best_a is None:
        best_a = _anti_sine(n, 0.7071067811865475)
        best_score = _internal_score(best_a)

    return best_a, best_score


def _softmax_weights(c, beta):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    cmax = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax) or cmax <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(c.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(c.size)

    x = float(beta) * (c - cmax)
    x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(x)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.ones(c.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(c.size)
    else:
        w = w / s

    w = 0.5 * (w + w[::-1])
    s = float(w.sum())
    if np.isfinite(s) and s > 1e-300:
        w = w / s
    else:
        w = np.ones(c.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(c.size)
    return w


def _weighted_gradient(a, w):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    L = w.size
    if n == 0 or L == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    need = n + L - 1
    N = _next_pow2(need)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fy = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], N)
    h = np.fft.irfft(fa * fy, N)

    idx = (L - 1) - np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    T = h[idx]
    return 2.0 * T


def _random_perturb(a, seed=0, sigma=0.2):
    a = _normalize(a)
    if a is None:
        return None
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    newa = a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), size=a.size))
    return _normalize(newa)


def _lse_optimize(a0, budget_s, seed=0, label="lse"):
    a = _normalize(a0)
    if a is None:
        return None, float('inf')
    if float(budget_s) <= 1e-6:
        return a, _score_normalized(a)

    start = time.monotonic()
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = _score_normalized(best)
    n = a.size
    iters = 0
    no_improve = 0
    hard = start + float(budget_s)

    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        return best, best_score

    print(f"[{label}] n={n} start={best_score:.9f}")

    targets = (0.15, 0.4, 0.05, 1.0, 0.8, 2.0, 0.02)

    while time.monotonic() < hard:
        c = _fft_convolve(a)
        c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
        if c.size == 0:
            break
        cmax = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(cmax) or cmax <= 1e-300:
            break

        if no_improve < 8:
            beta_factor = 10.0
        elif no_improve < 22:
            beta_factor = 22.0
        else:
            beta_factor = 48.0

        beta = min(beta_factor / cmax, 20.0 * beta_factor * float(n))
        if not np.isfinite(beta) or beta <= 0.0:
            beta = 12.0 * float(n)

        w = _softmax_weights(c, beta)
        grad = _weighted_gradient(a, w)
        if grad.size != n:
            break

        g = grad - float(np.mean(grad))
        gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
        if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax <= 1e-18:
            break

        chosen = None
        chosen_score = best_score

        for target in targets:
            if time.monotonic() >= hard:
                break
            step = float(target) * (g / gmax)
            step = np.clip(step, -3.0, 3.0)
            newa = a * np.exp(-step)
            newa = np.where(np.isfinite(newa), newa, 0.0)
            s = float(newa.sum())
            if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
                continue
            newa = newa / s
            sc = _score_normalized(newa)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < chosen_score:
                chosen_score = sc
                chosen = newa.copy()

        iters += 1

        if chosen is not None and chosen_score < best_score - 1e-13:
            a = chosen
            best = a.copy()
            best_score = chosen_score
            no_improve = 0
            if iters % 100 == 0:
                print(f"[{label}] n={n} it={iters} score={best_score:.9f}")
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve % 12 == 0:
                p = _random_perturb(a, int(seed) + iters, 0.25)
                if p is not None:
                    ps = _score_normalized(p)
                    if np.isfinite(ps) and ps < best_score - 1e-12:
                        a = p
                        best = a.copy()
                        best_score = ps
                        no_improve = 0
                        print(f"[{label}] random n={n} it={iters} score={best_score:.9f}")
            if no_improve > 120:
                break

    print(f"[{label}] n={n} done score={best_score:.9f}")
    return best, best_score


def _add_candidate(cands, a, score, name):
    arr = _normalize(a)
    if arr is None:
        return
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        score = _internal_score(arr)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    cands.append((float(score), len(cands), str(name), arr))


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total = max(120.0, float(budget_s))
    hard = t0 + total - 30.0
    seed = int(seed)

    candidates = []

    M = 5
    D = M + 1

    if total > 1900.0:
        lengths = [2048, 4096, 8192, 16384]
    elif total > 1000.0:
        lengths = [2048, 4096, 8192]
    else:
        lengths = [2048]

    family_by_n = {}
    family_score_by_n = {}

    for n in lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > hard - 600.0:
            break
        if n <= 4096:
            maxfev = 600
        elif n <= 8192:
            maxfev = 400
        else:
            maxfev = 250

        starts = _make_family_starts(D, seed + n)
        a, sc = _optimize_family_internal(n, M, starts, maxfev)
        if a is not None and np.isfinite(sc):
            family_by_n[n] = a
            family_score_by_n[n] = sc
            _add_candidate(candidates, a, sc, f"family{n}")

    for n in lengths:
        if n not in family_by_n:
            a = _anti_sine(n, 0.7071067811865475)
            sc = _internal_score(a)
            family_by_n[n] = a
            family_score_by_n[n] = sc
            _add_candidate(candidates, a, sc, f"anti{n}")

    length_items = []
    for n in lengths:
        sc = family_score_by_n.get(n, float('inf'))
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            sc = 1e100
        length_items.append((float(sc), int(n)))
    length_items.sort()

    chosen = []
    if length_items:
        chosen.append(length_items[0][1])
    if lengths:
        largest = lengths[-1]
        if largest not in chosen:
            chosen.append(largest)
    for _, n in length_items:
        if len(chosen) >= 3:
            break
        if n not in chosen:
            chosen.append(n)

    if not chosen:
        chosen = [2048]

    if len(chosen) == 1:
        chosen_weights = [1.0]
    elif len(chosen) == 2:
        chosen_weights = [0.55, 0.45]
    else:
        chosen_weights = [0.45, 0.35, 0.20]

    for ci, n in enumerate(chosen):
        if time.monotonic() > hard - 160.0:
            break
        remaining = hard - time.monotonic() - 140.0
        if remaining <= 0.0:
            break
        budget_len = remaining * chosen_weights[ci]
        if budget_len < 20.0:
            continue

        initials = [
            family_by_n.get(n, _anti_sine(n, 0.7071067811865475)),
            _anti_sine(n, 0.7071067811865475),
            _anti_sine(n, 0.55),
        ]
        if len(initials) == 3:
            init_weights = [0.45, 0.30, 0.25]
        elif len(initials) == 2:
            init_weights = [0.65, 0.35]
        else:
            init_weights = [1.0]

        for ii in range(len(initials)):
            if time.monotonic() > hard - 120.0:
                break
            sub_budget = budget_len * init_weights[ii]
            if sub_budget <= 5.0:
                continue
            shape = initials[ii]
            if shape is None:
                shape = _anti_sine(n, 0.7071067811865475)
            a_lse, sc_lse = _lse_optimize(
                shape,
                sub_budget,
                seed=seed + 9000 + 1000 * n + ci * 10 + ii,
                label=f"lse n={n} s{ii}",
            )
            if a_lse is not None and np.isfinite(sc_lse):
                _add_candidate(candidates, a_lse, sc_lse, f"lse n={n} s{ii}")

    # Polish the current best if enough time remains.
    if candidates:
        candidates_sorted = list(candidates)
        candidates_sorted.sort()
        best_now = candidates_sorted[0][3]
        rem = hard - time.monotonic() - 90.0
        polish_budget = min(rem, 900.0)
        if polish_budget > 60.0:
            a_pol, sc_pol = _lse_optimize(
                best_now,
                polish_budget,
                seed=seed + 77777,
                label="polish",
            )
            if a_pol is not None and np.isfinite(sc_pol):
                _add_candidate(candidates, a_pol, sc_pol, "polished")

    # Add safe constants (score 2) as fallbacks.
    for nc in [1024, 2048, 4096]:
        _add_candidate(candidates, _constant_sequence(nc), 2.0, f"const{nc}")

    if not candidates:
        out = _constant_sequence(2048)
        return [float(x) for x in out]

    candidates.sort()

    best_out = None
    best_real = float('inf')
    small_eval_limit = 4

    for sc, cid, name, a in candidates[:10]:
        if time.monotonic() > hard - 5.0:
            break
        n = a.size

        # To keep the running time safe, direct evaluation via the official
        # evaluator is only used for modest lengths.  The FFT surrogate is
        # very close for valid non-negative sequences.
        if n > 8192:
            continue
        if n > 4096:
            small_eval_limit = 2

        real = _actual_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(real) and real < best_real:
            best_real = real
            best_out = a
            print(f"[final] {name} n={n} actual={real:.9f}")
            if best_real < 1.45:
                break
            small_eval_limit -= 1
            if small_eval_limit <= 0:
                break

    if best_out is None:
        best_out = candidates[0][3]

    out = _normalize(best_out)
    if out is None:
        out = _constant_sequence(2048)

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
